Let's design code that uses numerical methods to optimize sequences of modest length. It may find improvements beyond C if any. To increase chance, we can exploit the exact problem as a minimax and use "smooth maximum + L-BFGS/Adam".

Since we have 1500s, N=2048: one evaluation FFT 8192 maybe ~0.5ms? Python overhead ~2ms. Can do tens of thousands Adam steps. Enough for convergence? Maybe.

Let's define top-level helpers:

- `_fft_len(n)`: next power of two >=2*n.
- `_convolve(a, fft_len)`: c = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)**2, fft_len)[:2*n-1]
- `_soft_max_weights(c, gamma)`: m=c.max; exp=np.exp(gamma*(c-m)); w=exp/exp.sum(); return m + log(sum)/gamma? Actually soft_max value = m + log(sum_exp)/gamma. weights=exp/sum_exp.
- `_gradient_soft(a, w, gamma?)`: dL = 2*corr(w,a) with corr = irfft(rfft(w,L)*conj(rfft(a,L)))[:n]. Return dL.
- `_score(a)`: exact score using np.convolve or FFT. Use FFT for speed.
- `_projection_simplex(a, target_sum=1)`: We can implement exact projection onto nonnegative simplex using sorting algorithm (top-level). But if we fix sum=1, after gradient update, need project to {a≥0,sum=1}. Euclidean projection: sort v descending, find ρ such that sum(max(v-κ,0))=target_sum. O(n log n), maybe heavy but okay every step? n=2048, 10k steps => 20M log maybe okay but Python sort 2048*10k=20M elements sorted? Actually each sort 2048 O(n log n) ~22k comparisons, 10k=>220M, maybe slow. Simpler clip+normalize faster and likely enough. Use `a1 = np.clip(a_upd, 0, 1e-300); a = a1 / a1.sum()`. This preserves positivity; not exact projection but works. However after many steps, may have negatives clipped, sum renormalized, effectively gradient step plus projection. Fine.
- `_adam_update(state, grad)` maybe inline in proposal.

Gamma schedule: We can use gamma = 50 * n? But if c values ~1/n, gamma*n gives numbers ~50. For N=2048, gamma=1e5. c-cmax differences maybe up to 1/n ~5e-4, gamma*diff ~50, good. Plateau differences smaller, weights spread. We can increase gamma over time: gamma = gamma0 * (1 + iter/1000) capped. Or use exact subgradient occasionally.

Learning rate: Gradient G = 2n*dL -2n*L (S=1). The scale of G? dL ~? w sum1, a sum1, dL_j ~2*mean a over k ~2*(1/n?)? Actually if w uniform over first n, dL_j ~2*(N-j)/(N^2?)? For C normalized a_i~1/N, dL_j~2*O(1/N). G ~2n*O(1/N)=O(1). L~1/N, second term ~2n*(1/N)=O(1). Gradient scale O(1). Adam normalizes, so learning rate 0.01-0.1 in parameter y? If updating a, after grad step a - lr*G, with G O(1), and a O(1/N), lr should be small ~1e-5? Since a_i ~1/N=5e-4; gradient O(1); direct step lr*G would blow. Implement:
```
def _soft_gradient(c, a, gamma, L):
   cmax=c.max()
   z=gamma*(c-cmax)
   ez=np.exp(z)
   sumez=ez.sum()
   w=ez/sumez
   Lval=cmax + np.log(sumentz)/gamma
   # dL = 2*corr(w,a)
   fta=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
   ftw=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
   corr=np.fft.irfft(ftw*np.conj(fta), L)[:n]
   dL=2*corr
   G=2*n*(dL - Lval*np.ones(n))
   return G, Lval
```
For c_n approach, gamma smaller; but c values small, gamma*(c-cmax) if gamma=10 and c~1/n, exponent tiny! Need gamma scaled. Use `gamma = 10 * n` in formula with c. Or use c_n with gamma=10. Let's use c_n to avoid huge gamma:
```
cn = c * n
maxc=cn.max()
ez=exp(gamma*(cn-maxc))
w=ez/sum
L_n = maxc + log(sum)/gamma
# dL_n = n * 2 corr(w,a)
G = 2 * (n * 2corr) - 2 * L_n
```
Check: f=2*L_n? Since exact T=2n*max c =2*max(cn). Smooth T=2*L_n. Gradient=2*dL_n. dL_n = n*2corr. So G=4n corr -2 L_n. This matches previous G=2n(2corr - L) =4n corr -2nL; L=L_n/n, so same.

Exact score helper:
```
def _exact_score(a):
   n=len(a)
   L=next_pow2(2*n)
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]
   s=a.sum()
   return 2*n*c.max()/(s*s)
```

Let's detail code:

Top-level imports: numpy as np, time. Maybe scipy? We won't use scipy to avoid overhead, but can if needed. The rules allow but no need.

Helper functions:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    p=1
    while p < m: p <<=1
    return p
```

```
def _central_binomial(n):
    a=np.empty(n)
    val=1.0
    for i in range(n):
       a[i]=val
       val *= (2*i+1)/(2*i+2)
    return a
```
Recurrence: C_{i+1}=C_i*(2i+1)/(2i+2). For i=0: C1=1*1/2=0.5. Good.

```
def _normalize(a):
   s=a.sum()
   if s <=0: return np.ones(len(a))/len(a)
   return a/s
```

```
def _fft_score(a, fft_len=None):
   n=len(a)
   if fft_len is None: fft_len=_next_pow2(2*n)
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, fft_len)[:2*n-1]
   # small imaginary discarded
   s=a.sum()
   if s < 1e-12: return np.inf
   return float(2*n*np.max(c)/(s*s))
```

```
def _soft_weights_and_value(c, gamma, fft_len, n):
   cn = c * n  # to scale
   mx = float(np.max(cn))
   z = gamma*(cn-mx)
   ez = np.exp(z)
   sumez = ez.sum()
   if sumez <= 0 or not finite: weights=np.zeros(len(c)); weights[np.argmax(cn)]=1; val=mx
   else:
      ez/=sumez
      val = mx + np.log(sumez)/gamma
   return ez, val
```
But if gamma large, z min may be -inf? np.exp handles. We can clip z to [-700,0].

```
def _soft_gradient(c, a, gamma, fft_len, n):
   # c length 2n-1
   cn = c*n
   mx=cn.max()
   z=np.clip(gamma*(cn-mx), -700, 0)
   ez=np.exp(z)
   sumez=ez.sum()
   if sumez <= 0:
      w=np.zeros_like(cn); w[np.argmax(cn)]=1; L=mx
   else:
      w=ez/sumez
      L=mx + np.log(sumentz)/gamma
   ftw=np.fft.rfft(w, fft_len)
   fta=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
   corr=np.fft.irfft(ftw*np.conj(fta), fft_len)[:n]
   # G = 4n corr - 2L
   G = 4.0 * n * corr - 2.0 * L
   return G, L
```
Wait G is gradient of f=2*L_n? If L is L_n, yes f=2L, G=2*dL_n. dL_n=n*2corr, so G=4n corr. Then minus scale? Since we fixed sum=1 via parameterization, no sum term in a-space? For f(a) with constraint sum=1 not included. If we optimize a directly with projection, gradient of f only is 4n corr? But because f is not scale-invariant? It is scale-invariant, gradient not zero; if we just project, we need not subtract sum term? Let's check: f(a)=2*max(n c(a)) =2n max c. It is homogeneous degree 2? max c degree 2, so not scale-invariant unless normalized? Actually T=2n max c / S^2. If we fix S=1 in parameterization, f(a)=2n max c on simplex. Gradient wrt a with S fixed includes constraint? If we optimize y with sum=1 exactly, a is a function of y; the gradient wrt y derived from f(a) without quotient because sum is automatically 1. For f(a)=2n max c (with a in simplex), gradient wrt a is 4n corr (if max scaled) no -2L term. The -2L term arises when f=2n max c / S^2 without fixing S. Since y fixes S=1, use f=2L_n (where L_n=n softmax c). So G_a=4n corr. Then G_y=a*(G_a - a·G_a) as usual for softmax. This may be better. Let's verify scale invariance: If a is on simplex, f=2*max(cn). The gradient of max c wrt a is 2corr (for exact max? for soft dL_n=n*2corr). So G=4n corr. Good. There is no constant term. In direct a-space with Eulerian projection, one might include scale term to handle renormalization, but log-space handles sum.

So helper returns `G=4*n*corr` and `L`.

But G can be large. For C plateau, corr_j =∑_{k=j}^{n-1} w_k a_{k-j}? If w uniform first n scaled? w sums 1. corr ~O(1/n). G~4n*1/n=O(4). Actually earlier G_y could be O(a*G)=O(0.01). Fine.

Exact score we will use `2n*maxc/s^2`. Since sum=1, 2n*maxc.

Parameterization: from y to a:
```
def _softmax_from_y(y, lo=-40, hi=40):
   y=np.clip(y, lo, hi)
   m=y.max()
   e=np.exp(y-m)
   s=e.sum()
   a=e/s
   return a
```
If all y very negative, clip handles.

Gradient y:
```
aG = G * a
mu = aG.sum()
Gy = a * (G - mu)
```
Because ∂a_i/∂y_j = a_i(δij - a_j). ∂f/∂y_j = ∑ G_i a_i(δij-a_j)=a_j G_j - a_j ∑ G_i a_i.

Adam update on y:
```
beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
m_hat = m/(1-b1**t)
v_hat = v/(1-b2**t)
y = y - lr * m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+eps)
```
Add noise: `y += noise_std * rng.normal(0,1,n)` occasionally.

Since G can have large components for small j, Adam normalizes. lr choose 0.05? Let's do dynamic: `lr = lr0 / (1 + t/2000)` with `lr0=0.03`. For long runs maybe decay.

We should monitor exact score every 20 iterations. Accept if exact score < best. Since y update may temporarily worsen exact but soft improves. We allow patience.

Potential issue: The softmax parameterization cannot represent zeros; but zeros allowed. Minimum positive ~e^-40≈4e-18, negligible. Good.

Initial y for C: `a0=_central_binomial(n); a0=_normalize(a0); y=np.log(np.maximum(a0, 1e-300))`.

Restart variations:
- pure C.
- C with smooth noise: generate `noise_std=0.1` times lowpass. For speed, can just add rng.normal(0,0.05,n) and maybe optimize. High frequency may hurt but could find better.
- C with parameter `shift`: `y += alpha * (np.arange(n)/n) - beta * (np.arange(n)/n)**2` with alpha in [-0.5,0.5], beta [0,0.5]. This smooth multiplicative.

But full optimization from C may be enough. We can run multiple seeds for N=4096: pure C, noise, smooth shifts.

Time management: We cannot know exact runtime. Use `time.monotonic` checks.

We can implement a function `_optimize_sequence(n, seed, time_limit, initial_y, noise_std=0.0, alpha=0.0, beta=0.0, print_interval=10000?)` top-level. It returns best_y, best_score, best_arr.

```
def _optimize_sequence(n, seed, budget, y0=None, noise_std=0.0, alpha=0.0, beta=0.0, verbose=False):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget
    if y0 is None:
       a0=_central_binomial(n); a0=_normalize(a0); y0=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300))
    else: y0=y0.copy()
    if alpha or beta or noise_std:
       x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       if alpha: y0 += alpha*x
       if beta: y0 += -beta*x*x
       if noise_std: y0 += noise_std*rng.normal(size=n)
    a=_softmax_from_y(y0)
    best_arr=a.copy(); best_score=_fft_score(a)
    fft_len=_next_pow2(2*n)
    m_atom=np.zeros(n); v_state=np.zeros(n)
    t=0; patience=0; max_patience=2000? 
    gamma=20.0
    lr_base=0.05
    # maybe start gamma 10, increase to 100
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # gamma schedule: 10 + 90*min(1,t/5000)
       gamma = 10.0 + 90.0 * min(1.0, t/5000.0)
       fta=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
       c=np.fft.irfft(fta*fta, fft_len)[:2*n-1]
       G, L = _soft_gradient(c,a,gamma,fft_len,n)
       # gradient y
       aG=G*a
       Gy=a*(G - aG.sum())
       # sanitize
       Gy=np.nan_to_num(Gy, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
       t+=1
       m_atom=0.9*m_atom+0.1*Gy
       v_state=0.999*v_state+0.001*Gy*Gy
       mh=m_atom/(1-0.9**t)
       vh=v_state/(1-0.999**t)
       lr = lr_base / (1.0 + t/2000.0)
       step = mh/(np.sqrt(vh)+1e-8)
       # limit step norm? To prevent instability, clip step to [-0.5,0.5]
       step = np.clip(step, -0.5, 0.5)
       y0 = y0 - lr*step
       if t % 200 ==0:
          y0 += (noise_std/ (1+t/1000)) * rng.normal(size=n)  # anneal
       y0 = np.clip(y0, -40, 40)
       a = _softmax_from_y(y0)
       if t % 25 ==0:
          sc=_fft_score(a)
          if sc < best_score:
             best_score=sc; best_arr=a.copy(); patience=0
             if verbose: print(...)
          else:
             patience +=1
          if patience > 300: # maybe restart noise or break
             # add a perturbation
             y0 += 0.1*rng.normal(size=n)
             y0 = np.clip(y0,-40,40)
             a=_softmax_from_y(y0)
             patience=int(patience/2)
    # final exact
    return best_arr, best_score
```

Thus helper:
```
def _soft_gradient(c,a,fta,gamma,fft_len,n):
   cn=c*n
   mx=np.max(cn)
   z=gamma*(cn-mx)
   z=np.clip(z, -700, 0)
   ez=np.exp(z); s=ez.sum(); w=ez/s
   ftw=np.fft.rfft(w, fft_len)
   corr=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(ftw) * fta, fft_len)[:n]
   G=4.0*n*corr
   return G
```

Let's code:

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    budget_s = float(max(0.0, budget_s))
    total_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s - 8.0)
    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf
    plans = [(4096, 0.56, 0), (4096,0.16,2), (2048,0.14,1), (2048,0.08,3), (1024,0.06,1)]
    for n, frac, var in plans:
       if time.monotonic() >= total_deadline: break
       alloc = max(20.0, frac*budget_s)
       # ensure not exceed deadline
       if time.monotonic()+alloc > total_deadline:
          alloc = max(5.0, total_deadline - time.monotonic())
       try:
          seq, sc = _optimize_sequence(n, seed + n*7 + var*13, alloc, variant=var)
       except Exception:
          continue
       if sc < best_score:
          best_score=sc; best_seq=seq
          print(...)
    if best_seq is None:
       best_seq=_central_binomial(1024); best_seq=_normalize(best_seq)
    # scale? Our seq sum=1, within [0,1000]. To maybe avoid numerical issues, multiply by sqrt(1000)? Not needed. But if all values small, okay.
    # Check evaluate
    ev = globals().get('evaluate_sequence')
    if ev is not None:
       try:
          val=float(ev([float(x) for x in best_seq]))
          if np.isfinite(val): best_score=val
       except: pass
    return [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best_seq]
```

```
def _optimize_sequence(n, seed, budget, variant=0):
    if n <=0 or budget <=0: return central...
    y0 = _make_initial_y(n, variant, seed)
    a = _softmax_from_y(y0)
    best_y = y0.copy(); best_arr=a.copy(); best_score=_fft_score(a)
    fft_len = _next_pow2(2*n)
    m_adam = np.zeros(n); v_adam = np.zeros(n)
    t=0; patience=0
    print(f"[opt] n={n} variant={variant} start={best_score:.6f}")
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       fta = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
       c = np.fft.irfft(fta*fta, fft_len)[:2*n-1]
       c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
       s = a.sum()
       sc = 2.0*n*np.max(c)/(s*s)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-9:
           best_score=sc; best_arr=a.copy(); best_y=y0.copy(); patience=0
           if t % 1000 ==0: print...
       else:
           patience +=1
       if patience > 3000:
           y0 = best_y + 0.15*rng.normal(size=n)
           y0 = np.clip(y0, -35,35)
           a = _softmax_from_y(y0)
           m_adam[:] =0; v_adam[:] =0
           patience =0
       # gradient
       gamma = 20 + 180*min(1.0, t/20000.0)
       G = _soft_gradient(c,a,fta,gamma,fft_len,n)
       # If G all zero break
       Gy = a*(G - np.dot(a,G))
       Gy = np.nan_to_num(Gy)
       t +=1
       m_adam = 0.9*m_adam +0.1*Gy
       v_adam = 0.999*v_adam +0.001*Gy*Gy
       b1t=1-0.9**t; b2t=1-0.999**t
       step = (m_adam/b1t)/(np.sqrt(v_adam/b2t)+1e-8)
       step = np.clip(step, -0.3,0.3)
       lr = 0.03/(1.0 + t/3000.0)
       y0 = y0 - lr*step
       if t % 100 ==0:
           noise_amt = 0.02/(1.0 + t/2000.0)
           y0 += noise_amt*rng.normal(size=n)
       y0=np.clip(y0,-35,35)
       a=_softmax_from_y(y0)
    # final maybe evaluate a from best_y
    a_best=_softmax_from_y(best_y)
    sc_best=_fft_score(a_best)
    if sc_best < best_score: best_score=sc_best; best_arr=a_best
    return best_arr, best_score
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-function sequence minimizing

    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))**2

The code uses a scale-invariant log-sum parameterization, FFT-based exact
convolutions, and Adam optimization of a smoothed maximum.  Several starting
families and sequence lengths are tried.  A central-binomial initial profile
is included because it has the empirically best known large-n shape for this
problem; local Adam refinement and a few cheap global retweaks are applied to
try to beat the reference bound.
"""

import time
import numpy as np


def _next_pow2(m: int) -> int:
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _central_binomial(n: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return [1, 1/2, 3/8, 5/16, ...] of length n."""
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = 1.0
    for i in range(n):
        a[i] = v
        v *= (2.0 * i + 1.0) / (2.0 * i + 2.0)
    return a


def _normalize(a: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(len(a), dtype=np.float64) / float(len(a))
    return a / s


def _softmax_from_y(y: np.ndarray, lo: float = -38.0, hi: float = 38.0) -> np.ndarray:
    y = np.clip(np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64), lo, hi)
    m = float(np.max(y))
    e = np.exp(y - m)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(len(y), dtype=np.float64) / float(len(y))
    return e / s


def _fft_convolution(a: np.ndarray, fft_len: int = None) -> np.ndarray:
    n = len(a)
    if fft_len is None:
        fft_len = _next_pow2(2 * n)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, fft_len)[:2 * n - 1]
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def _fft_score(a: np.ndarray, fft_len: int = None) -> float:
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    if fft_len is None:
        fft_len = _next_pow2(2 * n)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, fft_len)[:2 * n - 1]
    mc = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mc):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mc / (s * s))


def _soft_gradient_from_convolution(c: np.ndarray, a: np.ndarray, fta: np.ndarray,
                                    gamma: float, fft_len: int, n: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Gradient of the scaled smooth maximum objective on the simplex.

    For a on the simplex, the soft objective is F = 2 * soft_max(n * c).
    If W_k are softmax weights of n*c, then
        dF/d a_j = 4 n sum_k W_k a_{k-j},
    computed as an FFT cross-correlation.
    """
    cn = c * float(n)
    mx = float(np.max(cn))
    z = gamma * (cn - mx)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    ez = np.exp(z)
    sz = float(np.sum(ez))
    if not np.isfinite(sz) or sz <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(cn)
        w[int(np.argmax(cn))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = ez / sz

    ftw = np.fft.rfft(w, fft_len)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(ftw) * fta, fft_len)[:n]
    corr = np.nan_to_num(corr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return 4.0 * float(n) * corr


def _make_initial_y(n: int, variant: int, seed: int = 0) -> np.ndarray:
    """Create log-sum coordinates for the central binomial profile and variants."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = _central_binomial(n)
    a = _normalize(a)
    y = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))

    if variant == 0:
        pass
    elif variant == 1:
        y += 0.08 * rng.normal(size=n)
    elif variant == 2:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        y += 0.35 * x - 0.20 * x * x
    elif variant == 3:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        y -= 0.45 * x * (1.0 - x)
    elif variant == 4:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        y += 0.25 * np.sin(np.pi * x)
    elif variant == 5:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        y -= 0.25 * x * x
    else:
        y += 0.05 * rng.normal(size=n)

    return y


def _optimize_sequence(n: int, seed: int, budget: float, variant: int = 0):
    """
    Adam optimization in log-softmax coordinates.
    Returns (best_array, best_score).
    """
    if n <= 0 or budget <= 0.0:
        a = _normalize(_central_binomial(max(1, n)))
        return a, _fft_score(a)

    y0 = _make_initial_y(n, variant, seed)
    a = _softmax_from_y(y0)

    best_y = y0.copy()
    best_arr = a.copy()
    best_score = _fft_score(a)

    epoch_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget) - 2.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 101)
    fft_len = _next_pow2(2 * n)

    m_adam = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v_adam = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    t = 0
    patience = 0
    max_patience = 2200

    start_score = best_score
    print(f"[opt] n={n} variant={variant} start_score={start_score:.6f} budget={budget:.1f}")

    while time.monotonic() < epoch_deadline:
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
        c = np.maximum(np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, fft_len)[:2 * n - 1], 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if s < 1e-12:
            break
        sc = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c) / (s * s))

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-10:
            best_score = sc
            best_arr = a.copy()
            best_y = y0.copy()
            patience = 0
            if t % 2000 == 0 or best_score < start_score - 1e-3:
                print(f"[opt] n={n} variant={variant} t={t} score={best_score:.6f}")
        else:
            patience += 1

        if patience >= max_patience:
            y0 = best_y + 0.18 * rng.normal(size=n)
            y0 = np.clip(y0, -38.0, 38.0)
            a = _softmax_from_y(y0)
            m_adam[:] = 0.0
            v_adam[:] = 0.0
            patience = 0
            continue

        gamma = 25.0 + 250.0 * min(1.0, float(t) / 20000.0)
        G = _soft_gradient_from_convolution(c, a, fa, gamma, fft_len, n)
        if not np.any(np.isfinite(G)):
            break

        mu = float(np.dot(a, G))
        Gy = a * (G - mu)
        Gy = np.nan_to_num(Gy, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

        t += 1
        m_adam = 0.90 * m_adam + 0.10 * Gy
        v_adam = 0.999 * v_adam + 0.001 * (Gy * Gy)

        b1t = 1.0 - 0.90 ** t
        b2t = 1.0 - 0.999 ** t
        step = (m_adam / b1t) / (np.sqrt(v_adam / b2t) + 1e-8)
        step = np.clip(step, -0.35, 0.35)

        lr = 0.025 / (1.0 + float(t) / 3000.0)
        y0 = y0 - lr * step

        if t % 120 == 0:
            noise = 0.018 / (1.0 + float(t) / 3000.0)
            y0 += noise * rng.normal(size=n)

        y0 = np.clip(y0, -38.0, 38.0)
        a = _softmax_from_y(y0)

    # Final exact check of the retained best state.
    final_a = _softmax_from_y(best_y)
    final_sc = _fft_score(final_a)
    if np.isfinite(final_sc) and final_sc < best_score:
        best_score = final_sc
        best_arr = final_a

    return best_arr, best_score


def _quick_variant_search(seq: np.ndarray) -> tuple:
    """Cheap deterministic retweaks of a promising sequence."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    best = seq.copy()
    best = _normalize(best)
    best_score = _fft_score(best)
    n = len(best)
    if n <= 1:
        return best, best_score

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    candidates = []

    for p in (0.97, 1.03, 0.99, 1.01):
        candidates.append(np.power(np.maximum(best, 1e-300), p))

    for q in (0.08, 0.18, 0.30, -0.08, -0.18, -0.30):
        candidates.append(best * (1.0 + q * x))

    for q in (0.12, 0.28, -0.12, -0.28):
        candidates.append(best * (1.0 + q * x * (1.0 - x)))

    for t in (-0.015, -0.007, 0.007, 0.015, 0.035):
        candidates.append(best + t * best[::-1])

    for k in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        weights = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        weights[: min(n, int(np.sqrt(n)))] *= 0.95
        weights[-min(n, int(np.sqrt(n))):] *= 1.0 + 0.05 * k
        candidates.append(best * weights)

    for cand in candidates:
        cand = np.nan_to_num(cand)
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(cand))
        if s <= 1e-300:
            continue
        cand /= s
        sc = _fft_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-9:
            best_score = sc
            best = cand.copy()

    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed: int = 42, budget_s: float = 1500.0, **kwargs) -> list:
    """
    Main entry point.  Searches multiple sequence lengths and variants,
    retains the best sequence found, and applies lightweight global retweaks.
    """
    budget_s = float(max(1.0, float(budget_s)))
    global_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s - 8.0)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf

    # Baseline: pure central-binomial profiles at several useful lengths.
    baseline_lengths = [1024, 2048, 4096]
    try:
        par = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if par.ndim == 1 and par.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(par)):
            par = np.clip(par, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(np.sum(par))
            if s > 0.01:
                baseline_lengths.insert(0, par.size)
    except Exception:
        pass

    seen = set()
    for n in baseline_lengths:
        n = max(1, int(n))
        if n in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(n)
        a = _normalize(_central_binomial(n))
        sc = _fft_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = a.copy()

    # If a parent sequence exists and is better, keep it.
    try:
        par = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if par.ndim == 1 and par.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(par)):
            par = np.clip(par, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = float(np.sum(par))
            if s > 0.01:
                psc = _fft_score(par)
                if np.isfinite(psc) and psc < best_score:
                    best_score = psc
                    best_seq = par.copy()
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Allocate search budgets among lengths / variants.
    plans = [
        (4096, 0.45, 0),
        (4096, 0.15, 2),
        (2048, 0.14, 0),
        (2048, 0.09, 4),
        (1024, 0.09, 1),
        (1024, 0.08, 3),
    ]

    for n, frac, variant in plans:
        if time.monotonic() >= global_deadline:
            break
        remaining = max(0.0, global_deadline - time.monotonic())
        if remaining < 30.0:
            break
        alloc = min(0.95 * remaining, max(45.0, frac * budget_s))
        alloc = max(15.0, budget_s * 0.01)

        try:
            seq, sc = _optimize_sequence(n, seed + 17 * n + 31 * variant, alloc, variant)
        except Exception:
            continue

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(seq)):
            continue
        seq = np.maximum(seq, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(seq))
        if s < 0.01:
            continue
        seq /= s
        sc = _fft_score(seq)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            print(f"[search] new best n={n} variant={variant} score={best_score:.6f}")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = _normalize(_central_binomial(1024))
        best_score = _fft_score(best_seq)

    # Final local retweaks.
    best_seq, best_score = _quick_variant_search(best_seq)

    # Safety validation; evaluate_sequence is assumed available if present.
    try:
        ev = globals().get("evaluate_sequence")
        if ev is not None:
            val = float(ev([float(x) for x in best_seq]))
            if np.isfinite(val):
                best_score = val
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Strictly enforce the evaluator's accepted domain.
    out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best_seq]
    s = sum(out)
    if s <= 0.01:
        out = [1.0] * 64
        s = float(len(out))
    return out
```
